   - B -> H -> E -> M
   - B -> H -> I -> J -> M
   - B -> H -> I -> P -> J -> M
   - B -> C -> H -> E -> M
   - B -> C -> H -> I -> J -> M
   - B -> C -> H -> I -> P -> J -> M
   - B -> C -> N -> O -> P -> J -> M

2. **Identify Critical Nodes:**
   - Removing node H will disconnect the paths from B through H to E, I, and subsequently to M.
   - Removing node I will disconnect the paths from B through I to J and P, and subsequently to M.
   - Removing node P will disconnect the paths from B through P to J and subsequently to M.

By removing any one of these nodes (H, I, or P), the paths between B and M will be completely disconnected. However, we need to find the minimum number of nodes to remove.

Upon closer inspection, removing node H alone is sufficient because it directly impacts multiple paths between B and M. Removing node H will disconnect all paths that go through H to E, I, and subsequently to M.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes to remove is 1, and the cut set is [H].

Nodes to remove: 1, Cut Set: [H]
